# Code map

Generated by `tools/ci/code_map.py build`; do not edit by hand. `python3 tools/ci/code_map.py check` fails when it is out of date.

How to use it: find the file here, run `python3 tools/ci/code_map.py find NAME` for the exact line (`path:line kind name`), then read only that range. The map has no line numbers, so it changes only when a file or symbol is added, removed or renamed.

426 source files; 0 without a purpose line (no module docstring or file header).

## `apps/audio_render/src/`

- **`audio_render.cpp`**: audio_render (D-34 M4 B1b): renders a sound, a clip or the audio tour of a build's audio table headless, frame by frame, through the real audio core and the headless backend, and writes what the format's test plan compares (format §11): the ring segment of every frame (L2), the ordered tone writes (L3), the core's state (L1) and the output model's samples as a WAV (L4).
   - namespace: `capsule::hal::system`
   - struct: `Ends, File, Hash, Run, RunEnds`
   - enum: `Mode`
   - function: `assert_failed, blank, json_field, json_text, list, main, note_ends, on_frame, open_out, parse, parse_number, parse_then, put, put_state, put_tone, render, tracks_exist, usage, wav_header`
   - method: `File::File, File::operator=, File::~File, Hash::add`
   - constant: `kBitsPerSample, kByteBits, kBytesPerFrame, kChannels, kClipHold, kClipPitch, kClipPriority, kClipVolume, kDataSizeOffset, kDefaultMaxFrames, kDefaultTail, kExitFailed, kExitOk, kExitUsage, kFirstCallFrame, kFmtBytes, kFnv64Basis, kFnv64Prime, kFormatPcm, kLineBytes, kLogSoundcntH, kLogWaveOffset, kNumberBase, kOffsetAlign, kOffsetBits, kOffsetByteRate, kOffsetChannels, kOffsetData, kOffsetFmt, kOffsetFmtSize, kOffsetFmtTag, kOffsetFormat, kOffsetRate, kPathBytes, kRiffOverhead, kRiffSizeOffset, kStateLineBytes, kTagBytes, kThenSeparator, kTourMaxFrames, kWavHeaderBytes, kWordBytes`

- **`bench_data.h`**: What the bench knows about the game's battle data besides capsule::battle::kData: the manifest's names (a scenario names species, moves, parties, ... as the manifest does) and the effect bank's debug tables (owner and hook names, the pc -> source line table).

- **`main.cpp`**: The battle bench (D-23 section 3.3): a headless host program, linked against one game's generated battle data, that runs a `capsule.bench/1` scenario and writes what happens as JSON lines (output.h): the transcript in the WP-2 golden format (tests/support/battle_transcript.h), and on request every draw from the battle stream, every effect VM host call with its source position, and every raw battle event.

## `include/capsule/battle/data/`

- **`ai.h`**: AI profiles (D-26 section 1, G1): how an AI party chooses its actions, as manifest data.

   - namespace: `capsule::battle`
   - struct: `TierData, TypeData`
   - enum: `Combine, TierKind`
   - constant: `kMaxTiers, kMaxTypesPer`

   - namespace: `capsule::battle`
   - struct: `Odds, Ratio`
   - constant: `kMaxBattlers, kMaxParties, kMaxPositions, kMaxSides, kNone16, kNone8`

   - enum: `StageKind, StatInfoFlag`

- **`field.h`**: Field rules (D-27 sections 1 to 4, packages F1 to F3): the structure's `lead` rule, the count of its timed field effects, of its field actions with their requirements' first growth rule and of its step effects, as data (tools/battlec/field.py).
   - namespace: `capsule::battle`
   - struct: `FieldRules`

   - enum: `OnTargetFainted`

   - enum: `Msg`

## `include/capsule/core/`

- **`endian.h`**: Little-endian load/store helpers (P-19).

## `include/capsule/screen/`

- **`arena.h`**: The screen arena (UM, D-33): one static block of memory (EWRAM on the GBA) that screens borrow their large state from while they are open, as the reference games allocate a screen's buffers from their heap when it opens and free them when it closes.

   - function: `active, begin, cancel, level, update`

   - namespace: `capsule::screen::layers`
   - struct: `LayerTemplate, Plan`

   - constant: `kLayers, kPaletteKeep, kPaletteRaw, kRowCells`
- **`screen.h`**: Full-screen scenes (UC, D-33): screens entered from the field, from scripts and from each other, each with its own background plan (capsule/screen/layers.h), sprites (capsule/screen/sprites.h), palettes, windows (text::window, mirrored into a layer with layers::put_window()), tasks (capsule/screen/tasks.h) and fades (capsule/screen/ fade.h), on a stack (a party list opens a summary; closing the summary sets the party list up again).

- **`script_combine.cpp`**: The engine's default combine script port (capsule/script/combine_port.h, B1): `combine open` picks the inputs from the party through the text box's paged menu (script::menu; "Combine" ends the picks once the method has enough), asks "A + B -> C?" with yes / no from the preview, then (yes) builds the combine::Combination over the attached State, lets the player pick the moves when the method's chooser is the player's (the candidates and "Done"), places the result and shows the State's queued texts (the logic's, the method's messages) one by one.
   - namespace: `capsule::script::combine`
   - struct: `Port`

- **`script_combine_none.cpp`**: The combine script port of a program without battle data (capsule/script/combine_port.h, B1): nothing is attached and nothing ever combines (`combine open|run ...` gives kCombineBack at once, the queries 0).
   - namespace: `capsule::script::combine`
   - function: `attach, open, poll, query, run, set_menus`

- **`battle_progress_tests.cpp`**: Tests for progression from the manifest (D-22 A8 / A12, D-23 section 2, E6c) against the battle data compiled from tests/data/battle_progress/battle.json, whose progression differs from the sample's (the engine's former fixed rules) in every part: a full move list asks which move to forget (learn.on_full request), creatures built without moves know the learnset's first moves (first_n), every usable battler of every party on the other side shares a fainted foe's experience (share party, receivers opposing_sides) and a record whose owner_id is not Setup::owner_id earns the ruleset's outsider bonus; new records come from a generation list of one draw (a 16-bit seed), constants (genes 7, two PP raises), a derived custom role (gender) and a derived trait the layout does not store (read through record_value()); and the ruleset's own pp_max (the move resource pp's max formula, D-25) adds half the base PP per raise.

- **`audio_core_stub.h`**: A stand-in for the audio core (capsule/audio/core.h) that the backend tests and the backends' smoke programs link instead of the real one (D-34 M4 B1b): it owns a ring, runs the DMA counter exactly as the format says (§6: -1 at each vsync(), at 0 reload 7 and restart the DMAs; the mix writes segment 0 when the counter is <= 1, else 8 - counter), and each vblank() writes a known pattern into the segment plus a few tone writes, so a test can predict every byte the backend captures.
   - namespace: `capsule::audio::stub_core`
   - function: `frames, init, pattern`
   - constant: `kMonoAt, kNoteEvery`

- **`__init__.py`**: The battle compiler's sections (tools/battle_compiler.py is the driver and the command line; it re-exports every public name of these modules):
   - constant: `_TOOLS`

- **`turn.py`**: Battle geometry and turn structure of the battle compiler: formats (positions, sides, reach masks, screen layout, stands), the engine's caps on parties, positions, sides, battlers and escape attempts, and the turn structure (D-22 A11, `turn`: action brackets, the speed tie rule, the end-of-turn phases, the hook dispatch order and the battle start's send-outs; capsule::battle::TurnRules).
   - function: `bracket, choice, compile_formats, compile_turn, source_list`
   - constant: `ADJACENT_SIDES, AT_MOST_ONCE_PHASES, BRACKET_MAX, BRACKET_MIN, DEFAULT_ITEM_BRACKET, DEFAULT_RUN_BRACKET, DEFAULT_SWITCH_BRACKET, DEFAULT_TURN, EACH_PHASES, END_PHASES, ESCAPE_ATTEMPTS_MAX, FORMAT_RANGES, MAX_BATTLERS, MAX_END_PHASES, MAX_PARTIES, MAX_POSITIONS, MAX_SIDES, MIN_SIDES, MOVE_PRIORITY, ON_TARGET_FAINTED, ONCE_PHASES, PHASE_EACH, SEND_ORDERS, SOURCES, SWITCH_IN_ORDERS, TIE_RULES`
